## Findings

### 🔧 🔴 F1 — Re-review records a re-check entry but never moves a marker

#### Description

Step 6 told the agent to re-check findings and stopped there; no outcome of a
re-check changed a state. `SKILL.md:38-43` @ `8c1e8e0`:

```
6. **Re-review.** On a PR update, open a new round: re-check every finding
   still open on the PR — approved, deferred, unverified, or awaiting triage
   — against the new revision, and add the findings the update introduced.
   Discarded and routed-out findings stay closed.
   **Ready when:** every open finding carries a re-check entry against the
   new revision, and every new finding carries a round ID.
```

The 🔧 transition existed only as a table row in the other file,
`report-format.md:68` @ `8c1e8e0`:

```
| 🔧 | Fixed — the author addressed it; names the revision, and the finding moves to the resolved section |
```

So four branches had no instruction: a finding found fixed, one still broken,
a ❓ settled by the new revision, and a ⏳ found fixed before any verdict.

Suggested fix: state the outcome of a re-check in the protocol itself.

#### Updates

  every change is documented and explained in Updates; no transition graph.

  its reason"; `SKILL.md:51-62` re-checks every finding whatever its state.

### 🔧 🔴 F2 — 🔧 Fixed findings fall outside both the open and the closed list

#### Description

Step 6 partitioned findings into re-checked and closed, and 🔧 was in neither
half. `SKILL.md:38-41` @ `8c1e8e0`:

```
6. **Re-review.** On a PR update, open a new round: re-check every finding
   still open on the PR — approved, deferred, unverified, or awaiting triage
   — against the new revision, and add the findings the update introduced.
   Discarded and routed-out findings stay closed.
```

A finding marked 🔧 in round 2 matched no clause in round 3, so a regression
had no detection path.

Suggested fix: drop the open/closed split and re-check everything.

### 🔧 🔴 F3 — ✅ had two trigger conditions and publishing was not a step

#### Description

`report-format.md:67` @ `8c1e8e0` defined the approved state by an act of
publication:

```
| ✅ | Approved — the user published it to the PR |
```

`SKILL.md:32-37` defined it by a verdict, and no step in the protocol ever
published anything, so an approval given in conversation had no rule.

Suggested fix: pick one definition.

### 🔧 🟡 F4 — Step 1's "Ready when" required a base defined for one target type only

#### Description

`SKILL.md:13-18` @ `8c1e8e0` admitted four target types and defined a base for
one:

```
1. **Scope.** Take the target from the user or confirm it explicitly — last
   commit, a named commit or range, a PR diff, or the working tree. Pin what
   was reviewed: the full SHA, or for an uncommitted working tree, the base
   SHA plus a note that the tree was dirty.
   …
   **Ready when:** the reviewed state and its base are pinned.
```

For a PR the base was genuinely ambiguous — merge-base or base-branch head —
and the choice changes the changed-path set feeding step 2.

Suggested fix: state the base per target type, and ask the user when it stays
ambiguous.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 10:43 — Round 1: raised.
- 2026-09-18 16:05 — User triage: valid; when ambiguity is high, ask the user.
- 2026-09-18 16:20 — Round 2: fixed by `92a04ba`. `SKILL.md:16-22` now names a
  base per target type — first parent, range endpoints, merge-base with the
  target branch, HEAD plus a dirty-tree note — and ends "One attempt to
  resolve an ambiguous target or base, then ask the user". ⏳ → 🔧. Residual
  gap for non-git file sets is raised fresh as F23.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Round 3: re-checked at `b1eb877`, the fix stands and
  widened — step 1 now covers untracked targets too, so the pin has a defined
  outcome for every target type (`SKILL.md:20-26`).
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`. Step 1 was rewritten
  this round and its criterion is back to "the reviewed revision and its base
  stand in the report header" (`SKILL.md:27-28`); it is reachable for every
  target because git is now mandatory (F26). Stays 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. Step 1 still names a
  base per target type, including "none for files reviewed as they stand", and
  its Ready-when asks for both (`SKILL.md:20-27`). Stays 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:45 — Round 6: re-checked at `3950512`. Step 1 names a base per
  target type and its Ready-when now asks for both in the report's *intro*
  (`SKILL.md:20-27`). Stays 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F6 — "one agent per rule" left granularity undefined and two checks unowned

#### Description

`SKILL.md:54-56` @ `8c1e8e0`:

```
- Dispatch a fleet for anything beyond a trivial diff: one agent per rule,
  each scoped to the paths that rule covers. A narrow, single-rule brief is
  what keeps an agent's verdict checkable.
```

"rule" was undefined (file or bullet?), "trivial diff" had no threshold, and
the purpose-fit and best-practice checks of `SKILL.md:50-51` belonged to no
rule, so no agent covered them.

Suggested fix: define the unit, give a threshold, add standing agents.

### 🔧 🟡 F7 — Fleet dispatch had no brief contract and an unestablished precondition

#### Description

`SKILL.md:79-82` @ `8c1e8e0` sent agents out with no stated brief, no naming
for their `.ai/` files, and an asserted — never checked — ignore status:

```
- **Hold the index, not the findings.** Fleet reports persist to files in an
  ignored working folder (`.ai/`), leaving `docs/` for the report alone; your
  context holds which agent produced which verdicts.

defeats `SKILL.md:66-67`.

Suggested fix: specify the brief, the file pattern, and the ignore check.

### 🔧 🟡 F14 — Report storage and commit cadence left four cases open

#### Description

`report-format.md:3-10` @ `8c1e8e0` fixed the report to `docs/` and required
it committed, without naming the branch, the missing-folder case, the
dirty-tree collision, or a commit point for a re-review round.

Suggested fix: name branch, folder creation, and every commit point.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 10:53 — Round 1: raised.
- 2026-09-18 16:05 — User triage: valid. Commit report and artifacts to the
  current branch, nothing else; create folders as needed; track the commits so
  a rewritten history cannot lose them.
- 2026-09-18 16:20 — Round 2: fixed by `92a04ba`. `report-format.md:3-15` now
  creates the folder when missing, commits "to the current branch after the

### 🔧 🟡 F20 — Every "Ready when" now depends on a PowerShell-only script with no fallback

#### Description

Four of the seven steps cannot be completed without running the script.
`SKILL.md:40-43` @ `92a04ba`:

```
   **Ready when:** every finding carries ID, severity, evidence, and a
   suggested fix, the dashboard is regenerated with
   [`Update-Dashboard.ps1`](Update-Dashboard.ps1), and the report is
   committed.
```

and the same clause at `:49-50`, `:61-62`, `:67-68`. The only invocation given
is `pwsh` (`report-format.md:38-40`):

```
pwsh -File <skill-dir>/Update-Dashboard.ps1 -Path <report.md>
```

An agent on a host without `pwsh` — a Linux or macOS runner, or a Windows box
carrying only Windows PowerShell, where the executable is `powershell` — has
no defined way to satisfy those criteria and no stated fallback.

Suggested fix: name the Windows PowerShell invocation alongside `pwsh`, and
say what to do where neither exists (write the dashboard by hand from the
finding headers, marking it hand-counted).

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 16:22 — Round 2: raised. Verified the skill mentions no other
  runtime: `pwsh` occurs once, in `report-format.md:39`.
- 2026-09-19 13:40 — User triage: valid. Resolved by rewriting the script for
  Node rather than by documenting a second PowerShell invocation: PowerShell
  is Windows-only, Node runs TypeScript directly, and a missing runtime is a
  question for the user, not a fallback.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: fixed by `b1eb877`. `Update-Dashboard.ps1` is
  deleted; `update-dashboard.ts` runs anywhere Node 24 or newer does, and the
  guardrail turns a missing or older runtime into a stop-and-ask
  (`SKILL.md:117-123`). ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Renamed from `R2-1` when the `R<n>-` scheme was dropped
  (F21). The rename is a one-off migration by user direction; the format's
  "never renamed" rule (`report-format.md:91-93`) holds from here on.
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`. The second script is

### 🔧 🟡 F21 — Triage verdicts carry no round tag, so the rounds enumeration under-reports

#### Description

The dashboard claims to enumerate "the rounds that moved a finding"
(`report-format.md:42-43` @ `92a04ba`), but only entries carrying both a round
tag and a state arrow reach it. `Update-Dashboard.ps1:121-129`:

```
    $roundMatch = [regex]::Match($body, $roundRx)
    $moveMatch = [regex]::Match($body, $moveRx)
    if ($roundMatch.Success -and $moveMatch.Success) {
```

Step 5 requires the move to be explained but never asks for the tag
(`SKILL.md:48-50`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding the user ruled on carries its state in the
   header and an Updates entry explaining the move, the dashboard is
   regenerated, and the report is committed.
```

and the format ties the tag to rounds only (`report-format.md:69-71`): "an
entry belonging to a round opens with `Round <n>:`". A verdict applied during
round 2's triage therefore moves a finding without appearing under Round 2.

Suggested fix: require every Updates entry — triage included — to open with
the tag of the round it happens in, and say so in step 5.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 16:23 — Round 2: raised. Confirmed against the script: an entry
  with an arrow and no `Round <n>:` prefix is parsed as an update and dropped
  from `Moves`.
- 2026-09-19 13:40 — User triage: valid, with a simpler resolution than the
  suggested fix: rounds are recorded only in per-finding Updates, and all IDs
  collapse into one `F` sequence.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: fixed by `b1eb877`. The enumeration the round
  tag fed is gone — `report-format.md:123-126` keeps the round record in the
  findings and states the dashboard holds no round history — so an untagged
  verdict can no longer under-report anything. ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Renamed from `R2-2`; this finding's own fix is what
  renamed the rest.
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`, the fix stands — rounds
  live only in Updates, and no aggregation depends on the tag.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. Rounds leave their trace

  Stays 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F22 — The ignore-entry instruction conflicts with the commit rule

#### Description

The dispatch guardrail tells the agent to edit the reviewed repository's
ignore file (`SKILL.md:114-116` @ `92a04ba`):

```
  output; your context holds which agent produced which verdicts. Confirm the
  folder is ignored before dispatch, and add the ignore entry when it is not,
  or the scratch lands in your commit.
```

The commit rule two bullets down excludes exactly that change
(`SKILL.md:118-120`):

```
- **Commit what the review produced, nothing else.** The report goes to the
  current branch, together with any artifact kept outside the ignored working
  folder; record every commit in the report header, because a reviewed branch
```

So the `.gitignore` edit either rides along against the rule or stays
uncommitted — and an uncommitted edit dirties a working tree that step 1 may
have pinned as the review target (`SKILL.md:19-20`).

Suggested fix: say where the ignore entry goes — its own commit made before
the review starts, named in the header's Commits list — or fall back to a
path the repository already ignores.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 16:24 — Round 2: raised.
- 2026-09-19 13:40 — User triage: valid. The report and its artifacts belong
  in a git-tracked `docs` folder committed to the current branch — no ignored
  working folder, so no ignore entry to write.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: fixed by `b1eb877`. `report-format.md:3-6`
  requires a tracked folder and forbids an ignored path, and the dispatch
  guardrail no longer touches `.gitignore` (`SKILL.md:112-116`). ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Renamed from `R2-3`.
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`, the fix stands — no
  ignore-entry instruction returned; mandatory git (F26) removed the last
  reason to reach for one.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. The report goes to a

  (`report-format.md:3-6`); no ignore-entry instruction remains. Stays 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F23 — Step 1's "Ready when" is unreachable for the non-git targets now admitted

#### Description

The skill widened its targets (`SKILL.md:11-12` @ `92a04ba`):

```
The target is any changeset or text — a commit, a range, a PR, a working
tree, a set of files. Nothing in the protocol assumes a PR.
```

Step 1 allows such a target to have no base — "none for files reviewed as they
stand" (`:19-20`) — yet its exit criterion still demands a revision
(`:23-24`):

```
   **Ready when:** the reviewed revision and its base stand in the report
   header.
```

and the header spec repeats it (`report-format.md:25-26`): "**Reviewed** — per
round, the pinned revision and the base it is read against". A file set
outside version control — plain documents, an unpacked archive — has neither,
so the step can never report ready.

Suggested fix: let the pin name a non-revision identity (the path set plus a
content hash or a date) and state that the base may be absent.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 16:25 — Round 2: raised. This round hit the case itself: the
  round-1 target was a directory read as it stands, recorded in the header as
  "no base".
- 2026-09-19 13:40 — User triage: valid. Where the target is not tracked, ask
  the user how changes should be tracked; no tracking at all — the content
  read whole each round — is a legitimate answer.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: fixed by `b1eb877`. Step 1 asks the question and
  admits a content pin, a date, or nothing (`SKILL.md:20-26`), and the header
  has a place for that answer (`report-format.md:26-28`). ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Renamed from `R2-4`. The storage half of the same case —
  where the report goes when there is no repository — is raised fresh as F26.
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`, and the mechanism
  changed. The fix this finding got in round 3 — ask the user how to track an
  untracked target — was retracted by user decision: git is mandatory, and
  step 1 stops and suggests `git init` instead (`SKILL.md:17-20`). The
  original gap stays closed, because no target without a revision is admitted
  any more. Stays 🔧 on a different fix.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. Git is unconditional in
  step 1, with `git init` as the expected outcome (`SKILL.md:17-20`), so the
  Ready-when is reachable for every admitted target. Stays 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F24 — The rounds enumeration repeats a finding per move and links GitHub-only anchors

#### Description

The enumeration emits one entry per move (`Update-Dashboard.ps1:162-168` @
`92a04ba`):

```
            foreach ($move in @($finding.Moves | Where-Object { $_.Round -eq $round })) {
                "[$($finding.Id)](#$($finding.Anchor)) $($move.From) $arrow $($move.To)"
            }
```

so a finding moved twice in one round — approved during triage, then fixed by
the same round's re-check — appears twice in the same line. The link target
follows one flavour of anchor only (`Update-Dashboard.ps1:19-20`):

```
    Heading links follow the GitHub heading-slug rule: lower-cased, every
    character outside [a-z0-9 _-] dropped, spaces turned into hyphens.
```

Those anchors resolve on GitHub but not in Obsidian, whose heading links use
the literal heading text — and `report-format.md` never mentions the flavour,
so a reader of the format cannot know.

Suggested fix: collapse a finding's moves within a round to first → last, and
note the anchor flavour in the format document.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-18 16:26 — Round 2: raised. The duplicate-entry case is reachable
  today; the anchor flavour is a documented limitation of the script only.
- 2026-09-19 13:40 — User triage: valid. The enumeration goes entirely; that
  data lives in Updates, and GitHub-style anchors are the default because
  plain markdown is what every renderer reads.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: fixed by `b1eb877`. The dashboard is one section
  per state with numbered links (`report-format.md:45-62`), so no finding can
  appear twice, and the anchor rule is spelled out in the format itself
  (`:56-58`). ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Renamed from `R2-5`.
- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: re-checked at `666bfae`, the fix stands — one
  linked section per state, anchors documented.

### 🔧 🔴 F25 — The script missed every state change because Updates entries wrap

#### Description

An Updates entry runs past one line in an 80-column report, so the round tag
and the state arrow land on different lines. The parser read one line at a
time (`Update-Dashboard.ps1:117-129` @ `92a04ba`):

```
    if ($null -eq $current) { continue }
    $updateMatch = [regex]::Match($line, $updateRx)
    if (-not $updateMatch.Success) { continue }
    $body = $updateMatch.Groups['body'].Value
    $roundMatch = [regex]::Match($body, $roundRx)
    $moveMatch = [regex]::Match($body, $moveRx)
    if ($roundMatch.Success -and $moveMatch.Success) {
```

so `$body` held only the entry's first line and no move was ever recorded.
Run against this very report at `92a04ba`, the dashboard came back with
correct tallies and no rounds enumeration at all, while nineteen findings
carried a `Round 2:` entry ending `⏳ → 🔧`. The feature the format promises —
"enumerates the rounds that moved a finding" (`report-format.md:42-43`) — was
dead on arrival.

Suggested fix: accumulate an entry across its continuation lines before
parsing the round tag and the arrow.

  the rebuilt report produced no rounds section.
- 2026-09-18 16:25 — Round 2: fixed by `d7ac5a8`. Entries are collected with
  their continuation lines and parsed after the scan; the rebuilt report now
  renders `- Round 2 — [F1](…) ⏳ → 🔧, …` for all nineteen moves, and the
  scratch fixture still parses. ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 13:46 — Round 3: re-checked at `b1eb877`. The parser this fixed
  is gone with `Update-Dashboard.ps1`; `update-dashboard.ts` reads no Updates

### 🔧 🟡 F26 — Report storage and commit cadence assume a repository step 1 no longer requires

#### Description

Step 1 admits a target git does not track (`SKILL.md:20-23` @ `b1eb877`):

```
   for files reviewed as they stand. Where git does not track the target, ask
   the user how the rounds should tell one revision from the next — a content
   pin, a date, or no tracking at all, the content read whole every round.
```

Every instruction about where the report lives assumes the opposite
(`report-format.md:3-6`):

```
The report is a markdown document in the reviewed repository, in a folder git
tracks — `docs/` at the root unless the user names another location; create
the folder when it is missing. The report is a deliverable, not scratch: it
never goes to an ignored path.
```

so does the cadence (`report-format.md:12-13`) — "Commit it to the current
branch after the initial write, after each triage batch, after each round, and
at the close" — and the guardrail repeats it (`SKILL.md:124-125`): "The report
and its artifacts go to the current branch". A review of loose documents has
no repository, no tracked folder and no branch, so the four "Ready when"
criteria that end in "the report is committed" (`SKILL.md:44`, `:51`, `:63`,
`:69`) cannot be met.

Suggested fix: extend the step-1 question to cover where the report goes when
the target sits outside a repository, and qualify the commit clauses — "where
the target is in a repository" — so an uncommitted report is a defined
outcome rather than a failed criterion.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Round 3: raised. The same gap as F23 on the storage side:
  F23 fixed the pin, the report's own home stayed repository-only.
- 2026-09-19 18:50 — User triage: valid, with a simpler resolution than the
  suggested fix: git is a mandatory requirement, and the round-3 "ask how to
  track changes" branch is retracted.
- 2026-09-19 18:55 — Round 4: fixed by `666bfae`. Step 1 stops and suggests
  `git init` where the target is not in a repository (`SKILL.md:17-20`), the
  intro narrows targets to git repositories (`:11-13`), and the header's
  Reviewed bullet drops the untracked case (`report-format.md:44-45`), so the
  storage and commit rules apply to every target the skill accepts. ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. Step 1 requires git and

### 🔧 🟡 F27 — The dashboard script deletes the document tail when no `##` heading follows

#### Description

The dashboard ends at the next `##` heading, and at end-of-file when there is
none (`update-dashboard.ts:137` @ `b1eb877`):

```
  if (end < 0) end = lines.length;
```

Findings below `## Dashboard` with no `##` heading between them are also
skipped by the parser, which treats that span as the dashboard's own output
(`:118`):

```
    if (start >= 0 && end < 0) continue; // the dashboard's own sections
```

The run therefore counts zero findings and overwrites them. Verified on a
scratch copy: a file holding `## Dashboard` followed directly by
`### ⏳ 🔴 F1 — …` and its Description came back as `**No findings** — the
review raised none.`, the finding gone, exit code 0, no warning.

Such a report is malformed — `report-format.md:64` puts findings under
`## Findings` — but the format never states the dashboard must be followed by
a `##` heading, and the cost of getting it wrong is a silently truncated
document.

Suggested fix: fail with the same clear message used for a missing
`## Dashboard` when no `##` heading follows it, rather than rewriting to
end-of-file.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 13:52 — Round 3: raised, reproduced against the committed script
  on a scratch file outside the repository.
- 2026-09-19 18:50 — User triage: valid, and widened: the structure is to be
  enforced deterministically by a scaffolding script, with the dashboard
  generator relying on it.
- 2026-09-19 18:54 — Round 4: fixed by `bb6a586`. `scaffold-report.ts` writes
  the mandatory structure and refuses to overwrite; `update-dashboard.ts`
  reads findings only from `## Findings` and fails with the missing part
  named — the `end = lines.length` fallback that truncated the document is
  gone (`update-dashboard.ts:146-152`). Re-ran the round-3 repro: the file is
  left byte-identical and the run exits 1. ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:20 — Round 5: re-checked at `3438c4f`. The structure checks

### 🔧 🟡 F28 — Step 4 never asks for the `Round 1:` entry the format requires of every finding

#### Description

The format makes the round entry unconditional (`report-format.md:123-124` @
`b1eb877`):

```
A round leaves its trace in the findings themselves: every finding carries a
`Round <n>:` Updates entry for that round, whether the round moved it or not.
```

Step 6 enforces it from round 2 on (`SKILL.md:61`): "**Ready when:** every
finding carries a `Round <n>:` Updates entry". Step 4, which writes round 1,
asks for no such entry (`SKILL.md:42-44`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding carries ID, severity, evidence, and a
   suggested fix, the dashboard is regenerated with
   [`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts), and the report is committed.
```

A report can pass step 4 with round 1 unrecorded, and nothing surfaces it
until round 2 re-checks findings whose history starts nowhere.

Suggested fix: add the `Round <n>:` entry to step 4's criteria, or say in the
format that the entry raising a finding counts as its round entry.

### 🔧 ⚪ F29 — The fleet artifact path uses two placeholders the skill never defines

#### Description

`SKILL.md:112-114` @ `b1eb877`:

```
- **Hold the index, not the findings.** Fleet reports persist beside the
  review report — `<report-stem>/round-<n>/<rule>.md`, the folder created as
  needed — and are committed with it; your context holds which agent produced
```

Neither placeholder is defined anywhere in the skill. `<report-stem>` occurs
once; the format names the report `yyyy-mm-dd_<name>_review.md`
(`report-format.md:6`) and calls no part of it a stem. `<rule>` names a file
after "one enforceable statement — a single bullet of an instruction file, or
one explicit question from the user" (`SKILL.md:76-77`) — a sentence, with no
rule for turning it into a file name.

Suggested fix: spell both out — the report's file name without `.md`, and a
short slug of the rule, e.g. `agents-self-sufficiency.md`.

### 🔧 🟡 F30 — A finding raised during triage has no defined round, and step 5 asks for no entry

#### Description

The format admits findings that arrive after the validation pass
(`report-format.md:134-137` @ `666bfae`):

```
Two kinds arrive after the pass: the ones the user brings in directly, and
the ones a question during triage surfaces. Both take the next free IDs
alongside the agent's own, carrying the same evidence and state — a later
arrival joins the current round rather than opening a new one.
```

and requires a round entry of every finding (`:142-143`):

```
A round leaves its trace in the findings themselves: every finding carries a
`Round <n>:` Updates entry for that round, whether the round moved it or not.
```

Triage sits between rounds — "Steps 5 and 6 are a loop" (`SKILL.md:74`) — so
"the current round" names nothing definite at that moment, and step 5's exit
criterion covers only the findings the user ruled on (`SKILL.md:52-54`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding the user ruled on carries its state in the
   header and an Updates entry explaining the move, the dashboard is
   regenerated, and the report is committed.
```

A finding raised during triage can therefore reach the report with no round
entry at all — the gap F28 closed for step 4, left open at step 5.

Suggested fix: name the round a triage-born finding joins — the one just
completed — and add the entry to step 5's criteria alongside the verdicts.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 18:58 — Round 4: raised.
- 2026-09-19 19:12 — User triage: valid. A finding raised during triage must
  get a defined round and step 5 must ask for its entry, consistent with
  rounds living only in Updates.
- 2026-09-19 19:13 — Round 5: fixed by `f604b11`. Step 5 now states that a
  finding the user brings in, or one a triage question surfaces, "takes the
  next free ID and joins the round just triaged; triage opens no round of its
  own" (`SKILL.md:51-53`), and its Ready-when asks that every finding raised
  during the triage carry "a `Round <n>:` entry for that same round"
  (`:54-57`). The format names the same round — "the round just completed, the
  highest the report records" (`report-format.md:139-142`). ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:45 — Round 6: re-checked at `3950512`. Step 5 still gives a
  triage-born finding the round just triaged and asks for its entry
  (`SKILL.md:51-57`); the format agrees (`report-format.md:140-145`).
  Stays 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:52 — Round 7: re-checked at `d73844f`. Triage-born findings
  keep their round (`SKILL.md:51-57`, `report-format.md:142-147`). Stays 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F31 — A finding header above `## Findings` is dropped without a warning

#### Description

The parser ignores everything before the findings section
(`update-dashboard.ts:131` @ `666bfae`):

```
    if (!heading || !inFindings) continue;
```

Inside the section, a header it cannot read is reported (`:135`):

```
      console.warn(`update-dashboard: ignoring malformed finding header, line ${i + 1}: ${line}`);
```

Above it, nothing is. A well-formed finding written into the header section —
right shape, wrong place — is skipped in silence, stays out of the dashboard,
and passes the structure check, because all three sections are present.
Verified on a scratch file: a `### ⏳ 🔴 F1 — …` heading placed under
`## Header` produced `**No findings** — the review raised none.` with exit
code 0 and no warning.

Suggested fix: warn on a well-formed finding header found outside
`## Findings`, in the same shape as the malformed-header warning.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 19:12 — User triage: valid concern, wrong action. A finding-shaped
  header outside the findings section is a structure error, not a warning: the
  script must exit non-zero naming the header and its line, so the agent has to
  fix the structure.
- 2026-09-19 19:13 — Round 5: fixed by `3438c4f`. The parser now collects such
  headers instead of skipping them (`update-dashboard.ts:133-140`) and throws
  before the write (`:160-163`); the contract states it in both places
  (`report-format.md:38-39` and `:81-83`). Verified on a scratch file: exit

- 2026-09-19 19:45 — Round 6: re-checked at `3950512`. A misplaced finding

### 🔧 🔴 F32 — A second `## Dashboard` heading duplicates the document instead of stopping the run

#### Description

The script takes the *last* `## Dashboard` it sees as the start of the block
it replaces, while the end stays at the first section that followed the first
one (`update-dashboard.ts:122-129` @ `3438c4f`):

```
      if (title === "Header") header = i;
      else if (title === "Dashboard") start = i;
      else if (start >= 0 && end < 0) {
        end = i;
        endTitle = title;
      }
```

A stray second `## Dashboard` below `## Findings` therefore leaves `start`
greater than `end`, and the splice re-emits everything between them
(`:167`):

```
  const updated = [...lines.slice(0, start), ...build(findings), ...lines.slice(end)];
```

Nothing catches it: the structure checks (`:154-163`) all pass, because a
`## Header`, a `## Dashboard` and a `## Findings` right after it are present.
That contradicts the guarantee the format gives
(`report-format.md:36-38`):

```
[`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts) reads findings only from
`## Findings`, and stops with a message naming what breaks it rather than
rewrite the document.
```

Verified on a scratch report written by `scaffold-report.ts`, one finding and
a stray `## Dashboard` appended: exit code 0, `Dashboard updated: 1 findings`,
the file grown from 433 to 674 bytes with the finding body and the empty first
dashboard duplicated — silent corruption of the deliverable, the failure mode
F27 closed for the tail.

Suggested fix: count the mandatory headings and treat a repeat of any of them
as a structure error — name the duplicate with its line and exit 1 before the
write.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 19:22 — Round 5: raised, reproduced at `3438c4f`.
- 2026-09-19 19:38 — User triage: valid. A repeated `## Dashboard` must be a
  structure error, not silent duplication.
- 2026-09-19 19:40 — Round 6: fixed by `398c1c4`. The script collects every
  `##` heading (`update-dashboard.ts:120-123`) and rejects a repeat before the
  write — "'## Dashboard' appears twice in <path>, line <n>"
  (`:155-157`); the block's bounds are then taken from the validated sequence
  (`:172-174`). The format states it (`report-format.md:24-27`, `:83-85`).
  Verified on the scratch file of round 5: exit code 1, file byte-identical.
  ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 19:52 — Round 7: re-checked at `d73844f`. A repeated section is
  still rejected before the write (`update-dashboard.ts:167-173`). Stays 🔧.

  of the skill; the fix holds, state unchanged.

### 🔧 🟡 F33 — Findings are collected past `## Findings`, so a fifth section counts as findings

#### Description

The findings gate opens at `## Findings` and never closes
(`update-dashboard.ts:129` @ `3438c4f`):

```
      if (title === "Findings") inFindings = true;
```

The format admits exactly four parts (`report-format.md:24-25`):

```
Four parts, in this order: the `# Review — <target>` title, `## Header`,
`## Dashboard`, `## Findings`.
```

and promises that the script "reads findings only from `## Findings`"
(`:36-37`). A fifth section appended after the findings — notes, an appendix,
a scratch list — is neither rejected as a break of that contract nor kept out
of the count: its finding-shaped headers land in the dashboard as findings.
Verified on a scratch report with a `## Notes` section holding one
finding-shaped header appended after one real finding: exit code 0,
`Dashboard updated: 2 findings`, and the `## Notes` header listed under
`### 🔧 1 fixed`.

The mirror image of F31, covered by the same directive: right shape, wrong
place.

Suggested fix: stop collecting at the first `##` heading after `## Findings`,
and treat that section as a structure error — the contract allows no fifth
part.

  next `##` heading, and the contract must say whether a section may follow
  the findings at all.
- 2026-09-19 19:40 — Round 6: fixed by `398c1c4`. The contract now answers it:
  "Each appears once, in that order, and nothing follows the findings — a
  section repeated, misplaced, or added below them breaks the contract and
  stops the run" (`report-format.md:25-27`). The script rejects any other
  section — "unexpected section '## Notes' in <path>, line <n> — the report
  holds ## Intro, ## Dashboard, ## Findings and nothing else"
  (`update-dashboard.ts:153-155`) — and closes the findings gate at the next
  heading anyway (`:123`). Verified on the round-5 scratch file: exit code 1,
  file byte-identical. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F34 — Step 7 asks for a date where the header spec requires a timestamp

#### Description

The closing action names a date (`SKILL.md:70-71` @ `3438c4f`):

```
7. **Close.** The review ends when the user closes it. Set the header's
   status to closed with the date and the final revision, and leave every
```

The header spec is exact (`report-format.md:51-52`):

```
- **Status** — `active` while the review runs; at the close, `closed
  yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`.
```

"The date" drops the `hh:mm` the format demands, and the timestamp rule
(`:18-20`) reads as a rule for entries, not for this line. An agent working
from step 7 alone writes `closed 2026-09-19 at <revision>` and misses the
format.

Suggested fix: have step 7 say timestamp, or quote the shape the header spec
fixes.

  "Set the intro's status to `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` — the
  timestamp off the clock, the revision the last one reviewed" — and its
  Ready-when asks for both (`SKILL.md:70-75`), matching the intro spec
  (`report-format.md:53-54`). ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F35 — A malformed finding header inside `## Findings` drops the finding with a warning

#### Description

One section above, a finding-shaped header stops the run (F31); inside the
findings, a header the parser cannot read is only mentioned on stderr
(`update-dashboard.ts:134-137` @ `3950512`):

```
    if (!finding) {
      console.warn(`update-dashboard: ignoring malformed finding header, line ${i + 1}: ${line}`);
      continue;
    }
```

and the format blesses it (`report-format.md:80-82`):

```
findings; edit the section by hand and the next run overwrites it. Inside
`## Findings`, a `###` heading it cannot read stays out of the dashboard with
a warning on stderr — that header is malformed, fix it and run again.
```

The dashboard is the report's aggregate and the script its only author, so a
finding that misses it is invisible where the reader looks first — the
failure the strict handling of F31, F32 and F33 exists to prevent. The
trigger is a typo, not an exotic edit: a hyphen where the format wants an em
dash. Verified on a scratch report with two findings, the second written
`### ⏳ 🟡 F2 - Hyphen instead of em dash`: exit code 0, one warning on
stderr, `Dashboard updated: 1 findings`, and only F1 listed — the report now
claims one finding where it holds two.

Suggested fix: treat a `###` heading inside `## Findings` that does not parse
as a finding as a structure error too — name it with its line and exit 1 —
or, if non-finding `###` headings must stay legal there, say in
report-format.md what they are for and how the agent marks them.

- 2026-09-19 19:46 — Round 6: raised, reproduced at `3950512`.
- 2026-09-19 19:47 — User triage: valid. A malformed finding header must be a
  hard structure error — a warning with exit 0 loses a finding silently, the
  defect class the strict handling of F31 to F33 exists to prevent.
- 2026-09-19 19:48 — Round 7: fixed by `806d04c`. The parser collects the
  headers it cannot read (`update-dashboard.ts:142-145`) and throws before the
  write — "malformed finding header in <path> — line <n>: <header> — expected
  '### <state> <severity> <ID> — <title>'" (`:190-193`); the format drops the
  stderr-warning clause and lists the case among the breaks that stop the run
  (`report-format.md:38-45`, `:83-89`). Verified on a scratch report whose

### 🔧 ⚪ F36 — The `# Review — <target>` title is part of the contract but nothing checks it

#### Description

The structure contract counts four parts
(`report-format.md:24-25` @ `3950512`):

```
Four parts, in this order: the `# Review — <target>` title, `## Intro`,
`## Dashboard`, `## Findings`. Each appears once, in that order, and nothing
```

The validator knows three (`update-dashboard.ts:59`):

```
const SECTIONS = ["Intro", "Dashboard", "Findings"];
```

The title is written by `scaffold-report.ts:18` and never verified again, so
a report that loses it — a bad merge, a hand edit — passes. Verified on a
scaffolded scratch report with the `# Review — scratch` line deleted:
`Dashboard updated: 0 findings`, exit code 0.

Low: the scaffold puts the title there and nothing in the protocol removes
it. Raised because the same contract's other three parts are now enforced
deterministically, and a reader of `## Structure` would expect all four to be.

Suggested fix: check for a single `# ` title ahead of `## Intro`, with the
same message shape as the section errors.

#### Updates

  contract; missing or wrong must fail naming the expectation.

  heading (`update-dashboard.ts:59`, `:131-134`) and checks four things before

  `# Review — <target>`, and that it stands ahead of the intro
  (`:157-166`, `:182-185`). The contract names the title among the breaks
  (`report-format.md:24-30`, `:83-85`). Verified on scratch reports — no

### 🔧 🟡 F37 — The scaffold accepts a blank target and writes a title the dashboard script rejects

#### Description

The two scripts share one structure contract, and the scaffold can break it.
An empty target passes the nullish guard (`scaffold-report.ts:44-48` @
`d73844f`):

```
  const [path, target] = process.argv.slice(2);
  if (!path) throw new Error("usage: node scaffold-report.ts <report.md> [<target>]");
  if (existsSync(path)) throw new Error(`report already exists: ${path}`);

  mkdirSync(dirname(path), { recursive: true });
  writeFileSync(path, skeleton(target ?? "<target>"), "utf8");
```

`??` replaces `undefined`, not `""`, so the title becomes `# Review — `
(`:18`), which the other script refuses (`update-dashboard.ts:163-166`):

```
  if (!/^Review — \S/u.test(titles[0].text))
    throw new Error(
      `title in ${path}, line ${titles[0].line + 1}, reads '# ${titles[0].text}' — expected '# Review — <target>'`,
    );
```

Verified: `node scaffold-report.ts empty.md ""` exits 0 with
`Report scaffolded`, the first line reads `# Review — `, and
`update-dashboard.ts` on that same file exits 1 with
`reads '# Review —' — expected '# Review — <target>'`. An empty argument is
what a shell produces from an unset variable, and the agent is then told the
title is wrong rather than that the scaffold call was.

Suggested fix: treat a blank target like a missing one in
`scaffold-report.ts` — fall back to `<target>`, or refuse with a usage error
— so every document it writes passes the checks the other script makes.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 19:53 — Round 7: raised, reproduced at `d73844f`.
- 2026-09-19 20:26 — User triage: valid. The script that creates the document
  must never produce one its sibling rejects: a blank or whitespace target is
  refused with a non-zero exit and a clear message.
- 2026-09-19 20:35 — Round 8: fixed by `b4378ce`. `scaffold-report.ts` now
  trims the argument and stops on a blank one — `blank <target> — name what is
  under review, or omit the argument; '# Review — ' is a title
  update-dashboard.ts rejects` — and `report-format.md` records the refusal in
  the Structure section. Verified at `ee9e002`: `node scaffold-report.ts
  blank.md ""` and `"   "` both exit 1 writing no file, `"  skills/x  "`
  titles the document `# Review — skills/x`, and the omitted argument still
  writes the `<target>` placeholder — both files pass `update-dashboard.ts`
  with exit 0. ⏳ → 🔧.
- 2026-09-19 20:56 — Round 9: re-checked at `48e2f77`. `scaffold-report.ts`
  still refuses a blank target, and the Structure section still records the
  refusal. State unchanged.

### 🔧 ⚪ F38 — The state marker of a finding header is validated, the severity marker is not

#### Description

The parser takes any non-space token as the severity
(`update-dashboard.ts:55-57` @ `d73844f`):

```
const FINDING =
  /^(?<state>\S+)\s+(?<severity>\S+)\s+(?<id>[A-Za-z0-9-]+)\s+—\s+(?<title>.+)$/u;
```

and checks the state against the table while the severity goes unchecked
(`:150`, `:194`):

```
    if (!STATES.some((known) => known.marker === state)) unknown.push(`${finding.groups!.id} (${state})`);
```

```
    throw new Error(`unknown state marker on ${unknown.join(", ")} — see the states table in report-format.md`);
```

The scale is fixed to three markers (`SKILL.md:96-98`):

```
- Severity: 🔴 high breaks or violates a rule, 🟡 medium risks harm under
  realistic conditions, ⚪ low is polish or a suggestion. A style preference
  reported as 🔴 reads as noise.
```

and the header is "the single source of truth for state and severity"
(`report-format.md:98-99`). Verified: a finding written
`### ⏳ 🟠 F1 — Wrong severity marker` updates the dashboard with exit code 0
and is listed like any other.

Low: the dashboard groups by state only, so nothing downstream breaks — but
the one half of the header the format calls authoritative is checked and the
other is not.

Suggested fix: check the severity marker against the three of the scale, with
the message the unknown-state error uses.

  `SEVERITIES = ["🔴", "🟡", "⚪"]` and checks both markers of every header,

  script enforces and lists the marker break among the ones that stop the run.

  '🟠'`, `### 🟢 🟡 F1 — …` exits 1 with `line 11: state '🟢'`, both documents

### 🔧 🟡 F39 — A reused ID passes every check and the dashboard lists it twice

#### Description

The contract forbids reusing an ID (`report-format.md:120-122` @ `ee9e002`):

```
IDs run in one sequence across every round — `F1`, `F2`, …; a new round
continues from the next free number. An ID is never reused and never
renamed.
```

`update-dashboard.ts` enforces the state marker, the severity marker, the
header shape, the sections and the title, but records the ID without ever
comparing it to the ones already seen (`update-dashboard.ts:157-161`):

```
    findings.push({
      id: finding.groups!.id,
      state,
      anchor: slug(heading.groups!.rest),
    });
```

Verified at `ee9e002` on a scratch report holding `### ⏳ 🟡 F7 — First one`
and `### ⏳ 🔴 F7 — Second one with the same ID`: the run exits 0 with
`Dashboard updated: 2 findings` and writes

```
### ⏳ 2 awaiting triage

  1. [F7](#--f7--first-one)
  2. [F7](#--f7--second-one-with-the-same-id)
```

Two entries of the same name, which the reader cannot tell apart, and a
duplicate the agent has no signal to fix — the defect class every other
contract break now stops the run for. A copied finding block, the natural way
to write a new finding, is exactly how the duplicate arrives.

Suggested fix: collect the IDs in the parse loop and stop with a non-zero
exit naming the ID and both lines, as the misplaced- and malformed-header
checks do.

- 2026-09-19 20:42 — Round 9: fixed by `bda3832`. `update-dashboard.ts` keeps

### 🔧 ⚪ F40 — The dashboard of a review with no findings is script-only behaviour

#### Description

The script writes a sentence for the empty case
(`update-dashboard.ts:81-84` @ `ee9e002`):

```
  if (findings.length === 0) {
    block.push("**No findings** — the review raised none.", "");
    return block;
  }
```

The format defines the section as states and links only, with no empty case
(`report-format.md:70-73`):

```
It reads the finding headers of `## Findings` and rewrites everything between
`## Dashboard` and that heading as one section per state that carries
findings — marker, count, label — each listing its findings as numbered
links:
```

`SKILL.md:42-44` requires the report of an empty pass — "A pass that found
nothing still writes the report and says so" — but neither file says the
dashboard is where it says so, or in what words. With the guardrail added at
`ee9e002` — "Where [`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts) or
[`scaffold-report.ts`](scaffold-report.ts) disagrees with
[report-format.md](report-format.md) or this file, the script is what
changes" (`SKILL.md:133-137`) — an undocumented output reads as a script
defect, and the next agent may well "fix" the script by deleting the line.

Suggested fix: one sentence in the Dashboard spec stating that a report with
no findings carries `**No findings** — the review raised none.`

### 🔧 ⚪ F41 — The structure contract is enumerated twice and the two lists disagree

#### Description

The Structure section lists what breaks the contract
(`report-format.md:24-28` @ `ee9e002`):

```
Four parts, in this order: the `# Review — <target>` title, `## Intro`,
`## Dashboard`, `## Findings`. Each appears once, in that order, and nothing
follows the findings — a title missing or written another way, a section
repeated, misplaced, or added below the findings breaks the contract and
stops the run. Scaffold them with the script next to this file —
```

The Dashboard section lists it again, longer (`report-format.md:84-89`):

```
findings; edit the section by hand and the next run overwrites it. A broken
structure — a title missing, repeated, or not reading `# Review — <target>`,
a section missing, repeated, out of order, or added after the findings, a
finding header outside `## Findings`, a `###` heading inside them that does
not parse as one, a state or severity marker outside the scales — stops the
run with exit code 1, the offender named with its line and the document
```

The second list carries three breaks the first omits — a repeated title, a
missing section, an unknown state or severity marker — so a reader who stops
at the Structure section learns a contract narrower than the enforced one.
The drift is the predictable cost of two enumerations: both this round's
additions and round 7's landed in one list only.

Suggested fix: keep the enumeration in one place — the Structure section,
where the contract is defined — and have the Dashboard section say the script
stops on any break of it, with its line named and the document untouched.

### 🔧 ⚪ F42 — The ID sequence is a rule with no check: shape and order both pass unread

#### Description

Two rules govern IDs. The findings run in ID order
(`report-format.md:101-102` @ `48e2f77`):

```
Flat: one header per finding, all of them in a single section, in ID order,
first round to last.
```

And the IDs themselves are one `F<n>` sequence (`report-format.md:126-128`):

```
IDs run in one sequence across every round — `F1`, `F2`, …; a new round
continues from the next free number. An ID is never reused and never
renamed.
```

The contract list enforces neither (`report-format.md:49-50`):

```
- A state or severity marker falls outside the scales below.
- Two findings carry the same ID.
```

The script matches any word as an ID (`update-dashboard.ts:55-56`) and
compares IDs only against each other, for duplicates:

```
const FINDING =
  /^(?<state>\S+)\s+(?<severity>\S+)\s+(?<id>[A-Za-z0-9-]+)\s+—\s+(?<title>.+)$/u;
```

Verified at `48e2f77` on a scratch report holding `F9`, then `F2`, then
`XY-7`: the run exits 0 and the dashboard reproduces the document order,
`1. [F9]`, `2. [F2]`, `3. [XY-7]`. So a finding appended in the wrong place,
a typo'd number — `F4O` for `F40` — and an ID from another naming scheme all
reach the reader, and the dashboard presents them as the sequence the format
promises.

Lower severity than the duplicate ID of F39: nothing collides and no link
breaks, the index simply stops meaning what the format says it means.

Suggested fix: extend the contract list with an ID rule — IDs read `F<n>` and
rise down the section — and check both in the parse loop, naming the
offending line as the other breaks do.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 20:57 — Round 9: raised, reproduced at `48e2f77`.
- 2026-09-19 20:42 — User triage: valid. Enforce the sequence the format
  promises: IDs match `F<n>` and rise down the section, each violation a hard
  structure error naming the line and the expectation, with the contract list
  updated to match.
- 2026-09-19 20:50 — Round 10: fixed by `a49aeab`. `update-dashboard.ts`
  carries `ID = /^F(?<number>\d+)$/u`, records each finding's number in the
  parse loop and stops on an ID that is not `F<n>` — `finding ID out of shape
  … — an ID reads F<n>, as F7` — or that does not rise — `finding IDs out of
  order … F2 after F9 — they rise down the section`. The contract list gained
  "An ID reads anything but `F<n>`, or a lower one follows a higher". Verified
  at `a49aeab`: the `F9`-then-`F2` report exits 1 on order, the `XY-7` report
  exits 1 on shape, both byte-identical afterwards, and `F2`-then-`F9` exits 0.
  ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F43 — The contract covers headings only; the entries under them are unchecked and undeclared

#### Description

The Structure section closes its list of breaks (`report-format.md:54` @
`a49aeab`):

```
This list is the contract; nowhere else restates it.
```

Every bullet of that list is about a heading — title, sections, finding
headers, markers, IDs. Two other parts of the format read as mandatory and
appear nowhere in it. The intro's entries (`report-format.md:58-67`):

```
Top of the document, ahead of the dashboard:

- **Reviewed** — per round, the pinned revision and the base it is read
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies.
```

And a finding's two subsections (`report-format.md:115-116`):

```
Under it, two subsections, each a `####` heading so a renderer folds them
with the finding:
```

Verified at `a49aeab`: a report whose `## Intro` holds one line of prose and
whose single finding has neither `#### Description` nor `#### Updates` exits
0 — `Dashboard updated: 1 findings` — and the dashboard links it like any
other.

The cost is not cosmetic. The Updates subsection is where the round record
lives (`report-format.md:164-166`):

```
A round leaves its trace in the findings themselves: every finding carries a
`Round <n>:` Updates entry for that round, whether the round moved it or not.
That record is the only one the format keeps — the dashboard holds no round
history, so a round is read off the findings it touched.
```

A finding written without it loses the only history the format keeps, and
nothing tells the agent. Meanwhile the contract's closing sentence invites
the opposite reading: that whatever is not on the list is not enforced by
design. The format never says which reading is right, so an agent asking "is
a finding without Updates a structure error?" has no answer.

Suggested fix: decide it in the Structure section. Either extend the list and
the checks — an intro missing an entry, a finding missing `#### Description`
or `#### Updates` — or state that the contract holds the document's skeleton
and the entries under it are the agent's to keep.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 20:58 — Round 10: raised, reproduced at `a49aeab`.
- 2026-09-19 20:47 — User triage: valid. Extend the enforced contract to the
  mandatory sub-elements — the intro's four entries and each finding's
  `#### Description` and `#### Updates` — as hard structure errors naming the
  finding and what it lacks, with the contract list updated; scope the checks
  to what the format mandates and report any ambiguity rather than guess.
- 2026-09-19 21:00 — Round 11: fixed by `ae26be8`. `update-dashboard.ts`
  collects the intro's bold labels and each finding's `####` subheadings, then
  stops on `intro entry missing … **Commits** — the intro carries **Reviewed**,
  **Rules**, **Commits**, **Status**` or `finding subsection missing … F1 on
  line 23 has no '#### Updates'`. The contract list gained both bullets.
  Ambiguity resolved in the contract rather than guessed: the intro spec
  writes the entries as bullets while the scaffold writes bold labels on their
  own, so the bullet now reads "written as a bold label, on its own or opening
  a bullet" and the check accepts both. Presence only is enforced — the order
  of the two subsections, extra ones, and the entries' contents stay the
  agent's business, as the format mandates none of them. Verified at
  `ae26be8`: a finding without `#### Updates` exits 1 naming it, one without
  `#### Description` exits 1, an intro without `**Commits**` exits 1, all three
  documents byte-identical afterwards, a freshly scaffolded report and the
  bullet-form intro exit 0. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F44 — "Record each commit" cannot hold for the commit that carries the record

#### Description

The report must list every commit the review made
(`report-format.md:12-16` @ `ae26be8`):

```
Commit it to the current branch after the initial write, after each triage
batch, after each round, and at the close — the report and the artifacts the
review produced beside it, nothing else. Record each commit in the intro: a
reviewed branch is often rebased or force-pushed, and the SHAs are what let
you restore the document.
```

The intro spec repeats the requirement as a per-commit line
(`report-format.md:68-69`):

```
- **Commits** — one line per commit the review made: SHA, timestamp, what it
  carried.
```

A commit's SHA covers its own content, so the document committed in commit X
can never contain X's SHA. Every commit the review makes therefore leaves the
report one entry short of the rule, and the shortfall is not an oversight but
arithmetic. Step 7 asks for exactly the entry that cannot exist
(`SKILL.md:74-75`):

```
   **Ready when:** the intro's status reads closed with its timestamp and
   revision, the dashboard is regenerated, and the last commit is recorded.
```

At the close there is no later commit to carry the record, so the step's
"Ready when" is unreachable as written: an agent either stops with the
criterion unmet or commits again and lands one short once more.

The resolution is obvious once stated — each commit records the ones before
it, and the close either amends or accepts a trailing entry written after the
fact — but the format states none of it, and the restore argument it rests on
is what makes the missing SHA matter.

Suggested fix: say in the commits paragraph that an entry is written for the
previous commit, so the record trails by one; and let step 7 ask for the close
commit to be recorded by amending it or by a final commit whose own SHA the
user is told, rather than for a criterion the document cannot satisfy.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 21:06 — Round 11: raised, verified at `ae26be8`.
- 2026-09-19 20:52 — User triage: valid. Make the trailing explicit: each
  commit is recorded by the next one to touch the report, step 7 must not ask
  for the closing commit to record itself, and a record ending one short is
  acceptable. Keep the script presence-only.
- 2026-09-19 21:10 — Round 12: fixed by `b509364`. `report-format.md:16-19`
  now reads "The record trails by one: a SHA exists only once its commit does,
  so each commit is written into the intro by the next one to touch the report
  — the next triage batch, round, or close. When no later commit comes, the
  list ends one short; that is the rule holding, not a gap."; the intro spec's
  **Commits** entry says the list trails the branch by one; and step 7's
  "Ready when" now asks that "every commit before the closing one stands in
  the intro — the closing commit is the trailing entry the record never
  carries." The enforcement script is unchanged: presence of the entry, never
  its contents. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F45 — The commits guardrail restates the rule F44 corrected, without the correction

#### Description

The format now carries the rule with its qualification
(`report-format.md:12-19` @ `b509364`):

```
Commit it to the current branch after the initial write, after each triage
batch, after each round, and at the close — the report and the artifacts the
review produced beside it, nothing else. Record each commit in the intro: a
reviewed branch is often rebased or force-pushed, and the SHAs are what let
you restore the document. The record trails by one: a SHA exists only once
its commit does, so each commit is written into the intro by the next one to
touch the report — the next triage batch, round, or close. When no later
commit comes, the list ends one short; that is the rule holding, not a gap.
```

`SKILL.md` states the same rule, and the same rationale, without it
(`SKILL.md:140-143`):

```
- **Commit what the review produced, nothing else.** The report and its
  artifacts go to the current branch; record every commit in the report's
  intro, because a reviewed branch is often rebased or force-pushed and the
  SHAs are what let you restore the document.
```

"Record every commit in the report's intro" is the demand F44 showed cannot
be met — the commit carrying the record cannot name itself — and an agent
reading the guardrail alone is back where F44 started. The two sites also
repeat the rationale clause verbatim, which is how the structure contract
drifted before F41 single-sourced it: a rule written twice is corrected once.

Note the scope: the guardrail is not wrong about what to commit, only about
the record, and `SKILL.md` legitimately summarises the format elsewhere. The
defect is a summary that contradicts its source after the source moved.

Suggested fix: let the guardrail point rather than restate — "the report and
its artifacts go to the current branch; the intro records the commits, per
[report-format.md](report-format.md)" — leaving the trailing rule in the one
place that states it.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 21:14 — Round 12: raised, verified at `b509364`.
- 2026-09-19 20:56 — User triage: valid. Single-source the rule: the
  guardrail references the format's intro spec by relative link instead of
  restating it, so the trailing qualification cannot drift again.
- 2026-09-19 21:00 — Round 13: fixed by `e2fde5a`. `SKILL.md:140-143` now
  reads "The report and its artifacts go to the current branch; the intro
  records those commits, per [report-format.md](report-format.md#intro) — the
  cadence, the trailing rule, and why the SHAs matter live there, not here."
  The rule and its rationale exist once, in the format, exactly as F41 left
  the structure contract. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F46 — At the first write the commit record has nothing to carry, and no defined form

#### Description

The trailing rule settled which commit records which, and left the first
write with an empty record: the initial commit is written by the next one, so
at step 4's close the entry has no lines at all. `SKILL.md:45-47` @ `e2fde5a`
commits at exactly that point:

```
   **Ready when:** every finding carries ID, severity, evidence, a suggested
   fix, and a `Round 1:` Updates entry, the dashboard is regenerated with
   [`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts), and the report is committed.
```

What the entry carries meanwhile is undefined. The spec describes content
that does not exist yet (`report-format.md:71-73`):

```
- **Commits** — one line per commit the review made: SHA, timestamp, what it
  carried. The newest is written by the commit after it, so the list trails
  the branch by one.
```

And the scaffold leaves a placeholder in its place (`scaffold-report.ts:35-37`):

```
**Commits**

- \`<sha>\` · <yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm> · \`<subject>\`
```

So the agent reaches the first commit with three defensible moves and no rule
to pick between them: ship the placeholder — `<sha>` in a committed
deliverable — drop the bullet and leave the bold label alone, or invent a
line like "none yet". `update-dashboard.ts:65` checks the label only, so all
three pass.

The document already answers the sibling case: a dashboard with no findings
carries a defined line, `**No findings** — the review raised none.`
(`report-format.md:98-99`). The empty commit record deserves the same
treatment.

Suggested fix: give the empty record a line in the Intro spec — e.g. "Before
the first commit is recorded, the entry reads `- none recorded yet`" — and
make `scaffold-report.ts` write that instead of the `<sha>` placeholder, so
the scaffolded document is already correct on the point.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 21:00 — Round 13: raised, verified at `e2fde5a`.
- 2026-09-19 21:01 — User triage: valid. Define the empty state the way the
  dashboard's `**No findings**` line does: one defined line the scaffold
  writes and the format documents, enforcement scoped to what the format
  mandates.
- 2026-09-19 21:02 — Round 14: fixed by `ad7ebe7`. `report-format.md:71-75`
  now ends "before the first is recorded the entry carries one line in their
  place — `- none recorded yet, the record trails by one` — the line
  [`scaffold-report.ts`](scaffold-report.ts) writes into a fresh report", and
  `scaffold-report.ts:35-37` writes exactly that in place of the `<sha>`
  placeholder. Enforcement stays presence-only: the line is guaranteed by the
  script that creates it, as the no-findings line is guaranteed by the script
  that writes the dashboard. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F47 — A review with no base has no way to record one, and step 1 demands it anyway

#### Description

Step 1 admits a target that has no base and then asks for the base in the
intro (`SKILL.md:20-27` @ `ad7ebe7`):

```
   `git init` is the expected outcome. Pin the reviewed revision and the base
   it is read against: the first parent for a single commit, the endpoints
   for a range, the merge-base with the target branch for a PR, HEAD plus a
   dirty-tree note for an uncommitted tree, none for files reviewed as they
   stand. One attempt to resolve an ambiguous target or base, then ask the
   user — a wrong base silently changes the changeset under review.
   **Ready when:** the reviewed revision and its base stand in the report's
   intro.
```

For "files reviewed as they stand" the base is `none`, so the criterion asks
for something that does not exist, and nothing says what stands in its place.
The format's entry describes the base as always present
(`report-format.md:67-68`):

```
- **Reviewed** — per round, the pinned revision and the base it is read
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies.
```

And the scaffold's placeholder has one slot for it, with no form for the
absent case (`scaffold-report.ts:28`):

```
- Round 1 — \`<revision>\`, base \`<revision>\`; <clean or dirty tree>.
```

This is not the gap F4 closed — that one left the base undefined for four of
the five target types, and the enumeration above is its fix. What is missing
is how the fifth type records the `none` the enumeration assigns it.

This report hit the case in round 1 and improvised "no base, the target is a
file set rather than a diff"; another agent improvises differently, and a
reader cannot tell an absent base from a forgotten one.

Suggested fix: give the absent base a defined form, as the empty dashboard
(`report-format.md:98-99`) and the empty commit record
(`report-format.md:71-75`) now have — e.g. `base none — reviewed as it
stands` in the **Reviewed** entry, with the scaffold's placeholder naming the
option.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 21:02 — Round 14: raised, verified at `ad7ebe7`.
- 2026-09-19 21:05 — User triage: valid. Define the empty state the way F40
  and F46 did: one form in the format's **Reviewed** spec and in the
  scaffold, with step 1's Ready-when consistent with it.
- 2026-09-19 21:06 — Round 15: fixed by `0c7c1bb`. `report-format.md:68-71`

### 🔧 ⚪ F48 — The scaffold no longer writes only placeholders, but the Structure section still says so

#### Description

`report-format.md:37-40` describes what the scaffold produces:

```
It writes the skeleton — the intro's entries as placeholders, the two lower
sections empty — creates the folder on the way, and refuses to touch a file
that already exists or to write a blank `<target>`, which would title the
document in a way the dashboard script rejects.
```

Since the fix for F46, one of those four entries is not a placeholder but a
finished line the document is meant to carry
(`scaffold-report.ts:35-37`):

```
**Commits**

- none recorded yet, the record trails by one
```

An agent told every entry is a placeholder replaces that line with something
of its own at the first write — the very ambiguity F46 removed. The drift is
small and the same shape as F45: a summary left behind when its source moved.

Suggested fix: say what is true — the intro's entries as placeholders except
**Commits**, which carries its defined empty-record line until the first
commit is recorded.

### 🔧 🟡 F49 — The absent-base form is written out in three places at once

#### Description

The fix for F47 defined one form and then spelled it out at every site that
touches it. The format defines it (`report-format.md:68-71` @ `6a79bf0`):

```
- **Reviewed** — per round, the pinned revision and the base it is read
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies. Where there is no base —
  files reviewed as they stand — it reads `base none — reviewed as it
  stands`, so an absent base never looks like a forgotten one.
```

`SKILL.md:26-27` repeats the literal string:

```
   **Ready when:** the reviewed revision and its base stand in the report's
   intro — `base none — reviewed as it stands` where there is none.
```

And `scaffold-report.ts:28` carries a third copy:

```
- Round 1 — \`<revision>\`, base \`<revision>\` — or \`base none — reviewed as it stands\`; <clean or dirty tree>.
```

Reword the line once and two copies go stale — the defect F45 found in the
commit guardrail and F41 found in the structure contract, reintroduced in the
round that fixed F47. The script's copy is defensible, since the scaffold has
to write the words; the protocol's is not, and it is the one an agent reads
first.

Suggested fix: keep the form in the format's **Reviewed** entry and have step
1's Ready-when point at it — "the reviewed revision and its base stand in the
report's intro, an absent base in the form
[report-format.md](report-format.md#intro) defines".

### 🔧 ⚪ F50 — The scaffold offers the absent-base form in a shape that reads "base base none"

#### Description

`scaffold-report.ts:28` @ `6a79bf0` writes:

```
- Round 1 — \`<revision>\`, base \`<revision>\` — or \`base none — reviewed as it stands\`; <clean or dirty tree>.
```

The placeholder already carries the word `base` before the slot, and the
alternative repeats it inside the backticks, so filling in the second option
by substituting the slot — the obvious reading of a placeholder — produces
`base base none — reviewed as it stands`. Only an agent that notices the
duplication and deletes the first `base` gets the defined form
(`report-format.md:68-71`):

```
  stands`, so an absent base never looks like a forgotten one.
```

The line is also the one place in the skeleton that runs past the file's
column width, which is what hides the duplication.

Suggested fix: write the alternative without the second `base` — `base
\`<revision>\` — or \`none — reviewed as it stands\`` — or split the
placeholder over two lines, one per case.

### 🔧 🟡 F51 — The base slot points at forms the format never spells out

#### Description

Closing F49 and F50 left the scaffold with a bare slot and a comment that
promises more than the format delivers (`scaffold-report.ts:8-11` @
`2085f15`):

```
 * The skeleton follows report-format.md's Intro spec, which defines the
 * wording this file writes and leaves as slots: `<base>` takes either form
 * the spec gives, and **Commits** carries its empty-record line verbatim.
 * The spec is the source; change it there first.
```

The document the agent actually fills in now shows nothing but the slot
(`scaffold-report.ts:33`):

```
- Round 1 — \`<revision>\`, <base>; <clean or dirty tree>.
```

The spec, however, gives one literal form and not two
(`report-format.md:68-71`):

```
- **Reviewed** — per round, the pinned revision and the base it is read
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies. Where there is no base —
  files reviewed as they stand — it reads `base none — reviewed as it
  stands`, so an absent base never looks like a forgotten one.
```

The absent base is spelled out; the ordinary one — by far the common case —
is described in prose only. Before `510f7b9` the placeholder carried
`base \`<revision>\``, so the shape was visible in the document; now neither
the document nor the format states it, and "either form the spec gives"
names a second form that does not exist.

Suggested fix: give the ordinary case a literal in the same entry — "it reads
`base <revision>`, or `base none — reviewed as it stands` where there is
none" — which makes the scaffold's comment true and restores the example
without reintroducing the duplication F49 removed.

#### Updates

### 🔧 ⚪ F52 — The generated dashboard opens with a line the format never defines

#### Description

`update-dashboard.ts:76-81` builds the section it writes:

```
  const block = [
    "## Dashboard",
    "",
    "<!-- generated by update-dashboard.ts — do not edit by hand -->",
    "",
  ];
```

The format's dashboard spec enumerates what the section holds, and the
comment is not among it (`report-format.md:93-96`):

```
It reads the finding headers of `## Findings` and rewrites everything between
`## Dashboard` and that heading as one section per state that carries
findings — marker, count, label — each listing its findings as numbered
links:
```

The spec then closes the section against anything else
(`report-format.md:111-113`):

```
of the [structure contract](#structure) stops it before it writes. Nothing
else belongs in the section: state history lives in the findings' own
Updates.
```

Read literally, the script's own first line is what "nothing else belongs in
the section" forbids. This is the shape F40 corrected for the no-findings
line: behaviour that exists only in the script, with the docs-are-the-contract
guardrail (`SKILL.md:136-140`) saying the document decides. The cost is small
— this report carries the line at line 179, and an agent who deletes it as
foreign gets it back on the next run — but the contradiction is real.

Suggested fix: name the marker line in the dashboard spec, one clause beside
the no-findings line, or drop it from `build()`.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 22:12 — Round 17: raised, verified at `789fc97`.
- 2026-09-19 22:27 — User triage: valid. Document the marker rather than drop
  it — the F40 pattern, since the line tells a reader the section is derived.
- 2026-09-19 22:30 — Round 18: fixed by `726a398`. `report-format.md:105-106`
  now carries it: "The section opens with `<!-- generated by

### 🔧 ⚪ F53 — The skeleton description still calls **Status** a placeholder

#### Description

`report-format.md:37-41` describes the scaffold's output:

```
It writes the skeleton — the intro's entries as placeholders, except
**Commits**, which already carries its empty-record line, and the two lower
sections empty — creates the folder on the way, and refuses to touch a file
that already exists or to write a blank `<target>`, which would title the
document in a way the dashboard script rejects.
```

Two of the four entries are finished lines, not placeholders
(`scaffold-report.ts:40-44`):

```
**Commits**

- none recorded yet, the record trails by one

**Status** — active
```

`active` is exactly what the Intro spec requires while the review runs
(`report-format.md:80-81`):

```
- **Status** — `active` while the review runs; at the close, `closed
  yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`.
```

F48 corrected this sentence for **Commits** and left **Status** inside the
placeholder clause. An agent told to replace every placeholder rewrites a
line that is already correct — the same drift F48 closed, one entry over.

Suggested fix: state the two the agent fills — **Reviewed** and **Rules** —
instead of listing the exceptions.

### 🔧 ⚪ F54 — The contract list has no entry for a section that is not one of the three

#### Description

The structure contract carries one clause about sections
(`report-format.md:49`):

```
- A section is missing, repeated, out of order, or added after the findings.
```

`update-dashboard.ts:208-209` stops on a break that clause never names:

```
    if (!SECTIONS.includes(section.title))
      throw new Error(`unexpected section '## ${section.title}' in ${at} — the report holds ${expected} and nothing else`);
```

Verified: a `## Notes` section inserted between `## Intro` and `## Dashboard`
of a freshly scaffolded report exits 1 with `unexpected section '## Notes' in
<path>, line 20 — the report holds ## Intro, ## Dashboard, ## Findings and
nothing else`, the file byte-identical afterwards. The section is not
missing, not repeated, not out of order — the three that remain stand in
their order — and not after the findings.

The Structure preamble implies it (`report-format.md:27-29`): "Four parts, in
this order… Each appears once, in that order, and nothing follows the
findings." But F41 made the list the single authority
(`report-format.md:62`): "This list is the contract; nowhere else restates
it." An agent checking a failure against the contract finds no clause that
fits it.

Suggested fix: extend the clause — a section is missing, repeated, out of
order, added after the findings, or is not one of the three.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-19 22:12 — Round 17: raised, verified at `789fc97`.

#### Description

The structure is a contract, and [`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts)

Four of the breaks have no line to name, because the offender is an absence:
a missing title, a missing section, sections out of order, and a missing

with scaffold-report.ts
update-dashboard: intro entry missing in <path> — **Rules** — the intro
carries **Reviewed**, **Rules**, **Commits**, **Status**
```

Both exit 1 and leave the file untouched; neither carries a line, and neither
can. The script is right — `update-dashboard.ts:214-215` and `:249-253` name
what is missing instead — so this is the document overstating, the mirror of
the cases the docs-are-the-contract guardrail (`SKILL.md:136-140`) usually
settles the other way.

Suggested fix: qualify the clause — the offender named with its line where it
has one, named by what it is where it does not.

### 🔧 ⚪ F56 — Out-of-order sections have a line, and the sentence promises one

#### Description

The contract sentence, as F55 left it (`report-format.md:43-46`):

```
The structure is a contract, and [`update-dashboard.ts`](update-dashboard.ts)
enforces it. Each of these stops the run with exit code 1 and leaves the
document untouched, naming the offending line where there is one and the
expectation where the offender is an absence:
```

Out-of-order sections are neither: the offending heading sits on a line, yet
the message names none (`update-dashboard.ts:216-219`):

```
  if (sections.map((section) => section.title).join() !== SECTIONS.join())
    throw new Error(
      `sections out of order in ${path}: ${sections.map((section) => `## ${section.title}`).join(", ")} — expected ${expected}`,
    );
```

Verified on a freshly scaffolded scratch report with `## Intro` and
`## Dashboard` swapped — the misplaced `## Dashboard` stands on line 3:

```
update-dashboard: sections out of order in <path>: ## Dashboard, ## Intro,
## Findings — expected ## Intro, ## Dashboard, ## Findings
```

Exit 1, file untouched, no line named. The sentence's two branches — a line,
or an absence — leave this break in neither, so a reader who looks for the
line the contract promises finds nothing.

Suggested fix: name the first out-of-place section's line in the script, so
the sentence holds as written; the docs-are-the-contract guardrail
(`SKILL.md:136-140`) points that way. Widening the second branch to "where
the offender has no line of its own" is the smaller alternative.
