### 🔧 🟡 F57 — Only the script knows that fenced examples do not count

#### Description

`update-dashboard.ts:7-9` states the rule that makes the format's own
examples safe:

```
 * "## Findings" heading with one section per state present, each listing
 * links to its findings. Lines inside fenced code blocks are ignored, so
 * quoted examples never count as findings.
```

Neither instruction file says it. `report-format.md` never mentions a fence,
a code block, or a backtick outside its own examples, and the two clauses
that govern `###` headings (`report-format.md:52-54`) read as absolutes:

```
- A finding header stands outside `## Findings` — right shape, wrong place.
- A `###` heading inside `## Findings` does not parse as a finding header: a
  finding the dashboard drops is a finding the reader never sees.
```

The evidence rules require verbatim snippets (`SKILL.md:105-109`), and a
review of markdown quotes headings routinely — the format's own header
example at `report-format.md:130` is one. Fenced, it is invisible to the
script; unfenced, it becomes a finding. Verified on a scratch report whose
one finding quotes that example line without a fence:

```
update-dashboard: finding subsection missing in <path> — F1 on line 24 has
no '#### Updates'; F4 on line 30 has no '#### Description' — every finding
carries '#### Description' and '#### Updates'
```

Exit 1, file untouched — but the message blames the real finding for a
subsection it has and invents an F4 the agent never wrote. The rule that
would have prevented it is in a script header the agent has no reason to
read.

Suggested fix: add the exemption to the contract list — inside a fenced
block nothing is read as a heading, so evidence that quotes one is fenced.

#### Updates

  snippets are always fenced.
- 2026-09-20 18:38 — Round 21: fixed by `ed41e76`. `report-format.md:44-46`

### 🔧 🟡 F58 — An unclosed fence hides every finding after it, in silence

#### Description

The fence rule F57 added makes fences authoritative
(`report-format.md:44-46`):

```
enforces it. Fenced code blocks sit outside it: nothing inside a fence is
read as a heading or a finding, so quoted examples are always fenced — an
unfenced finding-shaped line is a finding, and validated as one.
```

Nothing checks that a fence closes. `update-dashboard.ts:126-133` toggles the
state and never inspects it again:

```
    const fenced = FENCE.exec(line);
    if (fenced) {
      const marker = fenced.groups!.marker;
      if (fence === null) fence = marker;
      else if (marker[0] === fence[0] && marker.length >= fence.length) fence = null;
      continue;
    }
    if (fence !== null) continue;
```

A fence left open inside a finding's Updates swallows the rest of the
document. Verified on a scratch report with two findings, the first ending in
an unclosed fence:

```
Dashboard updated: 1 findings in <path>
```

Exit 0. `F2` stands at line 43 of that file and appears nowhere in the
dashboard — the outcome the contract itself calls out one bullet later
(`report-format.md:56-57`): "a finding the dashboard drops is a finding the
reader never sees". Every other way to lose a finding is a hard error; this
one is silent, and an unbalanced fence is an ordinary authoring slip in a
report whose evidence is fenced snippets.

Suggested fix: after the parse loop, a fence still open is a structure break
— name the line it opened on and write nothing — with the clause added to the
contract list.

### 🔧 🟡 F59 — **Rules** is the one intro entry with no defined empty form

#### Description

Three of the intro's four entries define what they hold when there is nothing
to hold. The base (`report-format.md:76-79`):

```
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies. The base is written
  `` base `<revision>` `` where one exists and `base none — reviewed as it
  stands` where there is none, so an absent base never looks like a
  forgotten one.
```

The commits (`report-format.md:84-86`):

```
  the branch by one; before the first is recorded the entry carries one line
  in their place — `- none recorded yet, the record trails by one` — the line
  [`scaffold-report.ts`](scaffold-report.ts) writes into a fresh report.
```

And the dashboard, outside the intro (`report-format.md:113-114`): "A report
with no findings carries one line in their place: `**No findings** — the
review raised none.`"

**Rules** defines none (`report-format.md:80-81`):

```
- **Rules** — each instruction file paired with the paths its scope covers,
  plus the user's explicit review questions.
```

A repository with no `AGENTS.md`, no `CLAUDE.md`, no editor rule file, and a
user who asked no explicit question is ordinary, and the protocol still runs:
`SKILL.md:93-94` sends out "two agents no instruction file covers — one for
purpose fit, one for the ecosystem's best practices". Step 2's Ready-when
(`SKILL.md:34-35`) — "the report's intro pairs every changed path with every
instruction file whose scope covers it" — is then vacuously true, and the
agent improvises the entry or leaves the scaffold's placeholder
(`scaffold-report.ts:37-38`):

```
- \`<instruction file>\` → \`<paths in its scope>\` — <what it requires>.
- User's review questions — <the questions>.
```

Nothing catches the unfilled placeholder: the intro check is presence-only,
so a scaffolded report whose **Rules** still reads `<instruction file>`
passes. Verified on a scratch report — `Dashboard updated: 2 findings`,
exit 0, placeholder untouched.

Suggested fix: give the entry its literal beside its siblings — one line the
spec names, e.g. `- no instruction file covers the changed paths, and no
review questions were asked` — and word step 2's Ready-when so the empty case
resolves to it.

### 🔧 🟡 F60 — The Rounds section calls the findings' entries the only round record the format keeps

#### Description

`report-format.md:191-194` closes the format with an absolute claim:

```
A round leaves its trace in the findings themselves: every finding carries a
`Round <n>:` Updates entry for that round, whether the round moved it or not.
That record is the only one the format keeps — the dashboard holds no round
history, so a round is read off the findings it touched.
```

The format keeps a second one. The intro's **Reviewed** entry is per round
(`report-format.md:75-76`):

```
- **Reviewed** — per round, the pinned revision and the base it is read
  against, with a dirty-tree note where one applies.
```

And step 6 requires it of every round (`SKILL.md:69-71`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding carries a `Round <n>:` Updates entry, new
   findings continue the report's ID sequence, the round's revision stands in
   the intro, the dashboard is regenerated, and the report is committed.
```

So a round leaves two records — its revision in the intro, its effects in the
findings — and only the dashboard holds none. Read literally, the sentence
denies the intro's entry, and the agent that believes it drops the one line
that pins what the round actually read; step 6's Ready-when then fails for a
reason the format just argued against. Nothing catches the omission: the
intro check is presence-only, so a **Reviewed** entry that never grows still
passes.

Suggested fix: name both records and keep the contrast with the dashboard —
the round's revision lives in the intro, its effects in the findings, and the
dashboard holds no round history at all.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-20 18:58 — Round 23: raised, verified at `e38c811`.
- 2026-09-20 19:04 — User triage: valid. Reword so the sentence names both
  round records and their distinct jobs.
- 2026-09-20 19:08 — Round 24: fixed by `3682d34`. `report-format.md:191-195`
  now reads "A round leaves two traces, with distinct jobs. The intro's
  **Reviewed** entry pins what the round read — its revision and its base.
  Every finding carries a `Round <n>:` Updates entry for that round, whether
  the round moved it or not, and that is where the round's effects live. The
  dashboard keeps no round history, so a round is read off the intro and the
  findings it touched." The claim now matches the Intro spec and step 6's
  Ready-when. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F61 — A closed review has no way back: changes after the close hit a dead end

#### Description

Step 6 opens a round on any new change, unconditionally (`SKILL.md:60-61`):

```
6. **Re-review.** A round opens when new changes land in the reviewed area;
   confirm with the user when in doubt.
```

Step 7 ends the review (`SKILL.md:72-75`):

```
7. **Close.** The review ends when the user closes it. Set the intro's status
   to `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` — the timestamp off the clock,
   the revision the last one reviewed — and leave every finding in the state
   it reached; ⏳ and 📌 at the close are a result, not an oversight.
```

And the loop sentence makes step 7 terminal (`SKILL.md:81-82`): "Steps 5 and
6 are a loop: each round's findings are triaged, each change in the reviewed
area opens the next round, and the review runs until step 7."

Changes landing after a close — a fix pushed once the review is done, a
reopened PR — satisfy step 6's trigger against a report that step 7 has
already ended, and nothing says which way to go. The two resolutions are
incompatible and both defensible: reopen the report, which needs a status the
spec does not define (`report-format.md:91-92`) —

```
- **Status** — `active` while the review runs; at the close, `closed
  yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`.
```

— or open a new report, which the naming rule allows but which splits the ID
sequence and the finding history the format insists on keeping together
(`report-format.md:9-10`):

```
user-supplied name takes precedence. The name is fixed once chosen: every
later round appends to this same file.
```

Reopening silently overwrites the one record of when the review ended;
starting over silently drops the history. The other terminal cases are
defined — ⏳ and 📌 at the close are declared a result, the trailing commit is
declared acceptable — so this one reads as an oversight rather than a choice.

Suggested fix: give step 7 its re-entry branch — name what a post-close
change does (reopen the same report, with a defined status form that keeps
the close on record, or start a fresh one) — or declare the case out of scope
in one line, as the format does elsewhere.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-20 19:08 — Round 24: raised, verified at `3682d34`.
- 2026-09-21 08:57 — User triage: valid. Keep the history in one document:
  a change after the close reopens the review, the status returns to an
  active form with a dated note, and the sequences carry on; ask the user
  where the new work may be a different target.
- 2026-09-21 09:00 — Round 25: fixed by `8c7a4e6`. Step 6 now carries the
  branch (`SKILL.md:61-66`): "Changes landing after the close reopen the
  review instead of starting another: the same report continues, its status
  returns to the reopened form report-format.md#intro defines, and the round
  and ID sequences carry on — history stays in one document. Where the new
  work is really a different target, ask the user which it is." Its
  Ready-when adds "a round that reopened the review left the status in its
  reopened form" (`SKILL.md:76-77`), the loop sentence no longer ends at
  step 7 (`SKILL.md:86-88`), and `report-format.md:91-94` defines the form
  `active — reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>`. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F62 — Step 7 still says the closing commit is never recorded, which the reopen branch falsifies

#### Description

Step 7's Ready-when closes with an absolute (`SKILL.md:82-85`):

```
   **Ready when:** the intro's status reads closed with its timestamp and
   revision, the dashboard is regenerated, and every commit before the
   closing one stands in the intro — the closing commit is the trailing
   entry the record never carries.
```

"Never" held while a close was terminal. Step 6 now sends a review back into
the loop (`SKILL.md:61-63`):

```
   confirm with the user when in doubt. Changes landing after the close
   reopen the review instead of starting another: the same report continues,
   its status returns to the reopened form
```

A reopened review commits again, and that commit records the one before it —
the closing one. The format already states the rule conditionally
(`report-format.md:16-19`):

```
you restore the document. The record trails by one: a SHA exists only once
its commit does, so each commit is written into the intro by the next one to
touch the report — the next triage batch, round, or close. When no later
commit comes, the list ends one short; that is the rule holding, not a gap.
```

So the skill and the format now disagree: the format says the list ends short
only while no later commit comes, the protocol says the closing commit is one
"the record never carries". An agent that follows step 7 literally will skip
the closing SHA when it reopens — losing the pointer to the document's state
at the close, which is what the commit record exists for.

Suggested fix: qualify step 7 the way the format does — the closing commit is
the trailing entry until a later commit records it, which a reopen provides.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-21 09:12 — User triage: valid. Qualify the sentence — it states
  the trailing rule at close time; a reopen means the closing commit is
  recorded after all, by the next commit to touch the report.
- 2026-09-21 09:20 — Round 26: fixed by `bd1f5b8`. Step 7's Ready-when now
  reads "the closing commit trails, and the record picks it up only where a
  reopen brings a later commit" (`SKILL.md:84-85`), which is the format's
  trailing rule (`report-format.md:16-19`) stated at the close rather than a
  second, contradictory one. ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F63 — The reopened status claims the close stays on record, but it overwrites it

#### Description

The **Status** spec defines the reopened form (`report-format.md:91-94`):

```
- **Status** — `active` while the review runs; at the close, `closed
  yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`. A review reopened by a later change
  returns to `active — reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>`, so the
  close it came back from stays on record.
```

The status is one line: writing the reopened form removes the closed one, so
the close's timestamp and revision are gone. What survives is the word
"reopened" — that a close happened, not when or at what revision. The
trailing clause promises more than the form delivers.

The only other candidate is the commit record, and today it does not hold the
close either: step 7 calls the closing commit the entry "the record never
carries" (`SKILL.md:84-85`, raised as F62). Until that is settled, a reopened
report keeps no timestamp and no revision for its own close.

Suggested fix: either make the form carry what it claims — e.g. `active —
reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm after the close of yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`
— or drop the clause and let the closing commit, once F62 lets it be
recorded, be where the close lives.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-21 09:00 — Round 25: raised, verified at `8c7a4e6`.
- 2026-09-21 09:12 — User triage: valid. The reopened form must keep the
  close's timestamp and revision on record instead of overwriting them.
- 2026-09-21 09:20 — Round 26: fixed by `2e62364`. The **Status** spec now
  defines `active — reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>, after the
  close of yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`, "carrying the close it came back
  from instead of overwriting it" (`report-format.md:91-95`). ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 🟡 F64 — Only a change reopens the review; a verdict landing after the close has no path

#### Description

The close leaves findings open on purpose (`SKILL.md:78-81`):

```
7. **Close.** The review ends when the user closes it. Set the intro's status
   to `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` — the timestamp off the clock,
   the revision the last one reviewed — and leave every finding in the state
   it reached; ⏳ and 📌 at the close are a result, not an oversight.
```

So a closed report routinely carries ⏳ findings the user has not ruled on.
Ruling on them later is step 5, which states no precondition about the
review being open (`SKILL.md:50-55`):

```
5. **Triage.** Present the findings for verdict and answer the questions that
   precede one — an answer that changes a finding rewrites its description.
```

The way back, however, is written for changes only (`SKILL.md:61-66`):

```
   confirm with the user when in doubt. Changes landing after the close
   reopen the review instead of starting another: the same report continues,
   its status returns to the reopened form
```

A verdict is not a change landing in the reviewed area, so nothing tells the
agent what the status should read while it writes those verdicts, and the
report is left claiming `closed` while it is being edited.

The same gap shows in the commit record. The format commits the report
"after each triage batch" (`report-format.md:12-13`):

```
Commit it to the current branch after the initial write, after each triage
batch, after each round, and at the close — the report and the artifacts the
```

but step 7 now names the reopen as the only source of a later commit
(`SKILL.md:84-85`):

```
   closing one stands in the intro — the closing commit trails, and the
   record picks it up only where a reopen brings a later commit.
```

A post-close triage batch is a later commit too, and it would record the
closing one — which the sentence says only a reopen does.

Suggested fix: name the verdict path where the reopen branch is defined — a
verdict landing after the close reopens the review the same way a change
does, with the reopened status naming the triage as `<what landed>` — and
widen step 7's clause to any later commit, reopen or post-close triage.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-21 09:20 — Round 26: raised, verified at `2e62364`.
- 2026-09-21 09:24 — User triage: valid. Unify the trigger — any event that
  touches the report after a close reopens it, change or verdict, so
  "commit after each triage batch" stays true and no absolute says
  otherwise. The reopened status names what landed.
- 2026-09-21 09:28 — Round 27: fixed by `a285f00`. Step 6 now reads
  "Anything that touches the report after the close reopens the review
  instead of starting another — a change landing in the reviewed area, or a
  verdict on a finding the close left open" and separates the effects: "A
  change opens the round below; a verdict is step 5 and opens none"
  (`SKILL.md:62-69`). Step 5's Ready-when gained "a triage that reopened the
  review left the status in its reopened form" (`SKILL.md:58-59`). ⏳ → 🔧.

### 🔧 ⚪ F65 — The second close drops the first one back out of the status

#### Description

The reopened status now carries the close it came from
(`report-format.md:91-95`):

```
- **Status** — `active` while the review runs; at the close, `closed
  yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`. A review reopened by a later change
  returns to `active — reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>, after the
  close of yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`, carrying the close it came back
  from instead of overwriting it.
```

Closing that reopened review runs step 7 again, which writes the closed form
over whatever the line held (`SKILL.md:78-79`):

```
7. **Close.** The review ends when the user closes it. Set the intro's status
   to `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` — the timestamp off the clock,
```

The carried close goes with it: after close → reopen → close the status
shows the second close only, and nothing in the intro says the review was
ever closed before. The guarantee holds for exactly one cycle.

Suggested fix: say plainly that the status holds the latest transition and
name where the earlier ones live — the **Commits** record, whose lines say
what each commit carried — or let each close keep the chain it closes.

### 🔧 🟡 F66 — The loop sentence still names a change as the only thing that reopens

#### Description

Step 6 now reopens on any post-close event (`SKILL.md:62-66`):

```
   confirm with the user when in doubt. Anything that touches the report
   after the close reopens the review instead of starting another — a change
   landing in the reviewed area, or a verdict on a finding the close left
   open: the same report continues, its status returns to the reopened form
```

The sentence that closes the protocol was not brought along
(`SKILL.md:94-96`):

```
Steps 5 and 6 are a loop: each round's findings are triaged, each change in
the reviewed area opens the next round, and the review runs until step 7 —
which a later change reopens, back into the same loop.
```

"which a later change reopens" is exactly the narrow trigger step 6 stopped
using. An agent reading the protocol's summary keeps a closed review closed
when the only thing that lands is a verdict — the case the step above was
rewritten for.

Suggested fix: name the same trigger the step does, e.g. "which a later
change or verdict reopens".

### 🔧 🟡 F67 — A triage that reopens is not asked to record the close it came from

#### Description

The status now accumulates: the label line holds the current state and every
state left behind is written under the entry (`report-format.md:91-97`):

```
- **Status** — the label line holds the state now: `active` while the review
  runs, `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` at the close, `active —
  reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>` after a reopen. Every state the
  review leaves is added under the entry as its own line, oldest first —
  `- closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`, `- reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm,
  <what landed>` — so a second close never erases the first. A review still
  on its first `active` carries none.
```

Step 6's Ready-when asks for both halves (`SKILL.md:78-82`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding carries a `Round <n>:` Updates entry, new
   findings continue the report's ID sequence, the round's revision stands in
   the intro, a round that reopened the review left the status in its
   reopened form with the close it came from under the entry, the dashboard
   is regenerated, and the report is committed.
```

Step 5's, for the reopen a verdict triggers, asks for one (`SKILL.md:56-60`):

```
   **Ready when:** every finding the user ruled on carries its state in the
   header and an Updates entry explaining the move, every finding raised
   during the triage carries a `Round <n>:` entry for that same round, a
   triage that reopened the review left the status in its reopened form, the
   dashboard is regenerated, and the report is committed.
```

Since the reopened label form no longer carries the close it came from —
that clause moved into the history lines — a triage-triggered reopen done to
step 5's letter drops the close from the report altogether, which is the
loss the accumulation rule was written to prevent.

Suggested fix: give both Ready-whens the same clause, or state the obligation
once — the reopened status is the form the format defines, history lines
included — and have both steps point at it.

#### Updates

- 2026-09-21 09:28 — Round 27: raised, verified at `dc6b8bc`.
- 2026-09-21 09:32 — User triage: valid. Both reopen paths must preserve the
  history identically; single-source the definition if the clauses read as
  duplicates.
- 2026-09-21 09:35 — Round 28: fixed by `e62bd35`. Both Ready-whens now
  point at the canonical form instead of restating halves of it — step 5:

  `report-format.md#intro` defines" (`SKILL.md:58-61`), step 6 the same for

### 🔧 🟡 F68 — The first close has no state line to write, yet step 7 asks for one

#### Description

The status history is defined over every state the review leaves
(`report-format.md:91-97`):

```
- **Status** — the label line holds the state now: `active` while the review
  runs, `closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>` at the close, `active —
  reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm, <what landed>` after a reopen. Every state the
  review leaves is added under the entry as its own line, oldest first —
  `- closed yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm at <revision>`, `- reopened yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm,
  <what landed>` — so a second close never erases the first. A review still
  on its first `active` carries none.
```

The two line forms it gives are a close and a reopen. The state a *first*
close leaves is neither: it is the opening `active`, which carries no
timestamp and has no form here. The closing sentence covers the review
before that close — "still on its first `active`" — not the line the close
is supposed to write.

Step 7 then asks for that line outright (`SKILL.md:89-90`):

```
   **Ready when:** the intro's status reads closed with its timestamp and
   revision, the state it left stands under the entry, the dashboard is
```

At the first close — the common case — the criterion has no satisfiable
answer: an agent either writes an undated `- active` the format never
defines, or writes nothing and fails a Ready-when it was told to meet.

Suggested fix: scope the history to the transitions that carry a timestamp —
closes and reopens — and word step 7's criterion to match, e.g. the reopen
it closed stands under the entry where the review had been reopened.

#### Updates

  form so a first close records it like any other left state — the scaffold
  writes it, the format defines it, step 7 follows.
- 2026-09-21 09:42 — Round 29: fixed by `10417f9`. The **Status** spec now
  opens `active since yyyy-mm-dd hh:mm`, lists `- active since yyyy-mm-dd
  hh:mm` among the history lines, and states that "every left state carries
  the timestamp it started at. The opening line is stamped when
  `scaffold-report.ts` creates the report" (`report-format.md:91-100`).
  `scaffold-report.ts` stamps it from the clock at creation
  (`scaffold-report.ts:29-32`, `:45`), and step 7's Ready-when — "the state
  it left stands under the entry" — is satisfiable at the first close.
